Chapter 951: Do Not Bow Your Head. (1)

The disciples of Mount Hua, who had been lying around as if dead, all raised their heads with shining eyes.

They had heard about the situation while carrying people on carts, but there was a limit to what ordinary people knew. Mount Hua’s disciples naturally had to be curious about the details of the situation in the Yangtze River.

However, Chung Myung quickly turned around with a calm expression and just listened.

“No, well... they will figure it out.”

“... is that so?”

Chung Myung blew on the finger he used to clean his ear.

“Even so...”

“Ehh.”

Before Tang Gunak could say anything, Chung Myung waved his hand.

“The orders cannot be reversed, right?”

“Uh?”

“Who would take priority between those who went out to fight with their swords drawn in their own hands and those who were harmed without any reason?”

“...well...”

Tang Gunak couldn’t answer right away. Of course, it would be the latter.

“Even if you are interested in it, it is only after everyone else has been evacuated.”

Tang Gunak slowly nodded his head at Chung Myung’s words.

He was once again impressed by this young Taoist. Knowing something in their head and putting it into practice were two different things. Something that could change the landscape of martial arts was happening right around the corner, and it wasn’t something that could be done with normal patience to lose interest in it.

Chung Myung might be misunderstood because of his seemingly violent personality and exaggerated actions, but there aren’t many people in murim who are as patient as Chung Myung these days...

“But...”

“Uh?”

Chung Myung’s ears perked up. The hand which was lowered ahead was constantly moving, and his lips were also moving busily as if he wanted to say something.

Tang Gunak’s expression turned slightly shaky at the sight of the words and actions not matching.

Chung Myung hesitantly spoke.

“The... situation... well...”

“...”

“No! I am not curious about it! No need, but there could be damage during the transportation process... so... so...”

“You said you were not curious?”

“Eh! Who wouldn’t be curious? That is what everyone says!”

Tang Gunak sighed and shook his head.

Whatever...

“Yah! Whether the Namgung bastards get hurt or die, what does that have to do with me!”

Chung Myung, who suddenly lost his temper, turned around at the sahyungs and shouted.

“If you are resting, get up! Or I will have to wake you up!”

“... This bastard is now just taking his anger out on us.”

“Someday, I will get my chance...”

The disciples of Mount Hua stood up, grumbling. Even though they complained, they, too, wanted to move the refugees from the Yangtze River to a safe place as soon as possible.

Mount Hua could do nothing about those who had decided to live there. But, shouldn’t they at least do their best to help those who had made the big decision to move to Sichuan?

“Is there any problem with moving people to Sichuan?”

“Is there any problem?”

Tang Gunak answered calmly.

“First, getting out of the Yangtze River is a priority. Sending people to Sichuan can be done slowly. Thanks to the grains you brought and the grains flowing in from Plum Blossom Island, there will be no problems in feeding the refugees, so that is fortunate.”

“Then it is fine. Did you find land in Sichuan?”

“I am thinking about that.”

Tang Gunak frowned.

“Even if there is much land around, if possible, I would like to have them settle in Chengdu where the influence of the Tang family is strong....”

“Like to?”

“I think it will be difficult for them to adapt to city life. It would be even tougher for those living around the river.”

“Hmm.”

Chung Myung nodded as if he understood the point.

“Then I think we should settle in the Min River basin to the west of Chengdu. I believe that would be best for those who lived along the river. It isn’t a place that can be compared to the Yangtze River, but isn’t it still a river?”

Hearing that, Chung Myung smiled faintly.

The number of refugees moving around wasn’t small.

Although the sects had no great ties with common people, as the rulers of Sichuan, the Tang family, with their strong regional influence, would not want to lose the refugees who would undoubtedly support them.

Therefore, it was generally right to have people live in areas where their power was strong and try to gain profits there.

The Tang family also made many sacrifices to support them.

But now, Tang Gunak was prioritizing their lives over the interests of the Tang family. Even if it meant losing some influence, he was seeking a place that was most similar to where they lived.

‘This is how it should be.’

Chung Myung nodded vigorously, feeling happy for no particular reason.

“That sounds like a good idea.”

“Is that so? Hmm. I still don’t know. I think we should look for a better place.”

Three wrinkles formed between Tang Gunak’s eyebrows. Chung Myung eventually burst into laughter. Even when the Tang family elders were restraining him, he never showed such a look.

“The sect leader said that he would support them as much as possible in the name of the Heavenly Friends Alliance, so don’t worry too much. Getting them out of there is our priority.”

“Right.”

Tang Gunak nodded quietly.

That was when.

“Are you the people of Mount Hua?”

“The people of Mount Hua are here!”

The crowd of people came out of the place and surrounded Mount Hua’s disciples.

The refugees who were still staying in Wuhan discovered them and ran out barefoot.

“You must be having a hard time.”

“Oh my. Look at the dirt on your face.”

Those who ran out grabbed their hands, and the women wiped the faces of the Mount Hua disciples with their sleeves. The faces of the Mount Hua disciples turned bright red at the unexpected hospitality.

“N-no. You don’t have to do this...”

“What did we even do for you?”

However, for the refugees, it was a different story.

If they had tried to walk here on foot, they would have walked for days and might not have even reached their destination. With children, it would have taken more than ten days.

In this tense atmosphere of the Yangtze River, how could they not be grateful to those who came out to help them?

At that moment, someone gently tugged the sleeve of Jo Gul.

“Um... this...”

“Uh?”

Jo Gul turned around and blinked at what was in front of him.

“This is...?”

“It is a dumpling. It isn’t much, but please take this...”

“...”

Jo Gul absent-mindedly accepted the dumpling wrapped in cloth and wiped his nose.

“You didn’t have to do this....”

Of course, his body was very exhausted.

Even though he was a warrior who had mastered the art of martial arts and had now reached a level that most warriors couldn’t hope to achieve, it was not easy to pull a cart loaded with people and luggage and travel back and forth between Wuhan and the Yangtze several times a day.

But at this moment, all that fatigue and exhaustion felt like it had vanished.

“... I will eat this well.”

Yoon Jong tapped Jo Gul on the shoulder as he bowed his head.

“You will have this alone?”

“... Yes, I will eat this alone. I will not give this to sahyung.”

Normally, Yoon Jong would have grabbed Jo Gul by the collar, but now he just smiled broadly.

Someone said that righteousness means to give without any price.

But whenever he experienced such things, Yoon Jong couldn’t help but think that those words were wrong.

It wasn’t that there was no price; it was just that the price had a different meaning.

A warm welcome. A few words of thanks, a few dumplings—wasn’t that more than enough for them?

“Thank you.”

Baek Cheon bowed his head to them as their representative.

“If you think about it, the fact that you are going through all this hardship is because of the fault of those with swords. We cannot compensate for every loss, but we will do our best to try and help.”

“What do you mean? How is that Mount Hua’s fault?”

“Yes! I don’t know about other places, but I know how much Mount Hua and the Tang family have done for us. Even animals know grace, so we humans cannot be unaware of it.”

“Of course, we are grateful.”

Baek Cheon smiled broadly as he watched them wipe their eyes with their sleeves.

“Now, then let us go again...”

“If you have all rested, let us go, sasuk.”

“.. Uh?”

When he turned around, his sahyungs and sajils were already holding the cart’s handles.

“Now, maybe it is because you are old. You are not very nimble.”

“Even in this situation, the people of the Yangtze River are desperately waiting for us. We should move a little faster.”

“... these people?”

Baek Cheon glared.

It is death; it is death at this rate. Maybe get the alcohol bottle and have Chung Myung drink it up and put him to sleep; then they could rest a little too?

“Ah, the sun has already set. Will you go again?”

“Hahaha. What does the night have to do with us moving? There is someone waiting there for us, so we should go.”

Jo Gul?

“But you should rest a bit.”

“I have rested enough. This is better.”

Yoon Jong?

They were all turning into Chung Myung; their words and actions were not consistent.

“Ah, sasuk!”

“I get it! brats.”

Baek Cheon snickered and walked towards the cart. But soon, as if something came to mind, he stopped and turned to Chung Myung.

“Chung Myung...”

Baek Cheon was about to call Chung Myung but went silent.

Chung Myung, who had moved from the crowd, was looking somewhere.

South. The Yangtze River.

“....”

An unknown emotion was felt from that sight. Baek Cheon, who was contemplating calling him again, shook his head and slowly approached Chung Myung, placing a hand on his shoulder.

“Hmm?”

“... Let’s get going now, Chung Myung.”

“Ah, yes.”

Chung Myung nodded as if it were nothing. Baek Cheon didn’t bother to ask what Chung Myung was thinking. It wasn’t hard to guess, and there was no need to confirm it.

Baek Cheon glanced at Chung Myung’s back as he headed to the cart and quietly said,

“I have to take responsibility for my own mistakes.”

“... Uh?”

Chung Myung looked back. Baek Cheon glanced at him and calmly walked ahead.

“But then you stepped up for my mistake in the past....”

“....”

“So... back when I wasn’t able to properly lead the sajae and sajils as their great sahyung, you stepped ahead and changed everything.”

Baek Cheon paused for a moment but didn’t turn back.

“Actually, I felt really grateful.”

Then, as if remembering something, he turned and added,

“Just back then. Only back then. Not now.”

“Eh, this man!”

“Let’s go.”

Baek Cheon snickered and stood in front of the cart, grabbing the handle.

“We should finish this before the situation gets any worse. We can stop thinking about the aftermath then.”

“....”

Chung Myung looked at Baek Cheon and smiled.

“Dong-Ryong has grown up so much. You even know how to speak well to me.”

“I am originally a lot older than you, you brat! It was like that in the past, and it will continue to be so in the future!”

“What?”

Chung Myung clicked his tongue and turned to Tang Gunak.

“We will leave now. Among the people I brought earlier, there is a child who seemed very exhausted, so take a look at him.”

“Don’t worry, those learning medicines are already on it.”

“As expected of you, so flawless.”

Chung Myung smiled and jumped onto the cart.

“Okay, let’s get moving!”

“Euhh!”

This time, Mount Hua’s disciples also began pulling the cart without a word. The refugees cheered loudly as they watched the cart accelerate away in an instant.

In the midst of all this, Tang Gunak smiled and shook his head.

“This is bad.”

He never thought he would feel so proud of kids who hadn’t even lived half his age. If they kept going at it, he wouldn’t be able to take on the role of an adult.

“The Tang family cannot lose.”

He turned and shouted.

“Everyone, gather your things! We have to finish before Mount Hua comes back!”

“Yes, lord!”

Tang Gunak’s shoulders straightened more proudly than ever as he headed inside once again.

Chapter 952: Do Not Bow Your Head. (2)

Two days had passed.

During those two days, the Shaolin monks remained motionless, rooted to the spot where they had first sat.

No one sat down, nor did anyone leave. They simply stared at Plum Blossom Island and the fleet of the Four Precepts Alliance blocking the way, their eyes bloodshot.

A stillness had settled over them, yet their hearts were more tumultuous than ever.

In this world, everyone eventually grappled with the gap between reality and ideals. Navigating those moments and finding one’s place was what it meant to live.

However, the Shaolin monks of that day had not undergone such a typical process.

The threshold of Shaolin was too high for them to experience ordinary struggles, and the ideals they had learned were too lofty.

“... Abbot.”

One of the Shaolin monks, unable to bear it any longer, looked at the abbot with red eyes.

Hae Bang.

He had once visited Mount Hua to deliver an invitation to the Martial Arts tournament. His voice strained and escaped his lips.

“Are we... just going to watch?”

“....”

The abbot’s gaze remained fixed ahead, unmoving. It was unclear if he was listening.

But Hae Bang continued, undeterred.

“Are we really going to let the people of the Namgung family die like that?”

The abbot’s neck muscles twitched slightly. Hae Bang finally raised his voice.

“Abbot!”

Only then did the abbot slowly turn his head, meeting Hae Bang’s angry gaze.

“If we are, so what?”

“....”

“I will ask you in return. What would you like me to do?”

“....”

“Do you think we should fight the Four Precepts Alliance like this? Do you believe it is right to ignore the unfavorable battlefield and the forces being pushed back and jump into that river to die?”

Hae Bang bit his lip tightly.

The abbot’s words were not wrong. Jumping into that river now was no different from jumping into a fire with a bundle of firewood on your back.

But...

Hae Bang immediately looked straight at the abbot and said.

“Is that foolish?”

“Hae Bang!”

“Wasn’t it you, the abbot, who taught us that?”

At those words, the abbot went silent. His eyes wavered slightly.

“... Shaolin must protect the Central Plains. We must pursue the path of Buddhism, but we must not lose sight of the principles. You clearly said so.”

“...”

“Is this the way to protect Buddhism? Or is this the way to protect the principles?”

“Enough....”

“Was it just words? Abbot?”

“Can you not keep your mouth shut?”

The one who raised his voice was not the abbot but Beop Gye. He glared at Hae Bang with an angry face.

“You can just vent your anger. But the abbot must make a decision that will put the lives of everyone here at risk!”

Hae Bang bit his lips until they bled.

“Would you be able to send all your sahyungs and sajils here to their death for those principles? Can you confidently say those words?”

Hae Bang lowered his head, unable to answer.

The abbot’s eyes closed tightly.

In a situation where they could not do anything, everyone there was feeling twisted and horrible. It would only get worse as time passed. Just as a dark scar remained in the abscess that formed, perhaps even after the situation was done, that scar might never vanish.

‘... Jang Ilso.’

This was why Jang Ilso was fearsome.

Jang Ilso had already won.

Even if Shaolin jumped into the river and rescued the Namgung family with their people on their backs, the scars engraved on their hearts would never heal.

Shaolin, who had been moving forward without hesitation, armed with two weapons, Buddhism and Righteousness, might no longer exist from that moment.

Thinking that the entire situation was a scheme devised by just one person gave them chills and made it hard to breathe.

A helpless sigh escaped from the abbot’s mouth as he opened his eyes again.

He could feel it—the eyes of the disciples looking at him were no longer the same as before. The agony and distrust that had taken root in them were clearly revealed in their gazes.

And then, something fortunate happened to the abbot.

Jong Ri-Hyung of Kongtong, along with his men and the Gentle Crow Beggar of the Beggars Union, had arrived.

“What is the situation here?”

“... Amitabha.”

Jong Ri-Hyung spoke, unable to hide his frustration as he looked at the ships blocking Plum Blossom Island.

“On the island...”

“Yes.”

The abbot nodded weakly.

“There are survivors of Namgung there.”

At that moment, Gentle Crow Beggar’s eyes went dim.

Survivors.

It wasn’t wrong. But wasn’t the word “survivor” used to refer to the few who survived a force close to annihilation?

It was a very low tone.

“Those wicked people!”

They were also a leading sect, so it was obvious to them why the Four Precepts Alliance was blocking that place and keeping the Namgung family alive.

“Are we late?”

“... We came as fast as we could, but when we arrived, the Ten Thousand People clan had already taken over the river.”

“T-this is... how could the Ten Thousand People clan of Guangxu be here...”

Jong Ri-Hyung’s face darkened noticeably. Even the Ten Thousand People clan and the pirates alone were a strong force that wouldn’t be easy for those gathered there to deal with.

But if those people had secured ships and occupied the river, he couldn’t even imagine how many sacrifices they would have to make to break through that.

Jong Ri-Hyung asked.

“... Is it possible to break through?”

Gentle Crow Beggar answered calmly.

“Breaking through shouldn’t be that difficult. If we put all our strength into it, what problem would there be?”

“Then...”

Jong Ri-Hyung smiled as if he had found hope in those words. But at that moment, Gentle Crow Beggar suddenly dimmed the light.

“However, this isn’t a battle to break through the encirclement. We have to break through the place they are holding with our bare hands, reach the island, rescue the Namgung family, and cross back here.”

“....”

“That means we have to fight the same battle twice. The second time, we will be fighting with people on our backs and with our exhausted bodies.”

Sweat began to form on Jong Ri-Hyung’s forehead. Only then did he see how absurd the situation was.

“I-if we gather forces at Plum Blossom Island?”

“We will just get surrounded together. Are they just going to let us gather there?”

“...”

“Then how about we also get a ship?”

Gentle Crow Beggar snickered.

“Couldn’t the Hegemony Lord have thought of that? There probably won’t be a single ship within a few thousand miles from here. If we go further than this and get a ship, by the time the ship arrives here, the Namgung family will cease to exist.”

The abbot nodded. Although Gentle Crow Beggar certainly had a cynical side, he was able to grasp the situation quickly and accurately, as expected of an elder of the Beggars Union.

“Then what should we do?”

“What should we do?”

Gentle Crow Beggar looked at the deployed ships. To be exact, he was glaring at the people inside them.

“Either we squeeze out all the strength we have in us and risk our lives to rescue the Namgung... if not, we have to watch them die over there.”

“D-Die there...!”

Jong Ri-Hyung’s face turned pale.

‘W-what on earth is this?’

He had thought of many scenarios as he hurried to the Yangtze River, but he had never imagined a situation so absurd.

“W-what do you plan on doing, abbot?”

Jong Ri-Hyung ended up asking that.

He thought that this was something he couldn’t possibly judge. It was unbearable to break through that defense of ships and make so many sacrifices, and it was unbearable to watch the Namgung family die with his own eyes.

“... Amitabha.”

But the abbot shook his head.

“I don’t think it is something we can judge on our own.”

“T-then.”

“Since the Peng family hasn’t come, how about we make a decision after the Peng family lord arrives?”

“T-that seems like the right idea.”

Jong Ri-Hyung nodded.

But it wasn’t really a word he said after judging whether the abbot’s words were right or wrong. He was just relieved that the decision-making got postponed for the life of a sect, even by a little.

Actually, if one thought about it a little, it wasn’t something to be relieved about.

Jong Ri-Hyung looked around at the disciples, who were still confused because they hadn’t properly grasped the situation.

‘Damn it...’

There was too much time.

There were just three days left. It was too short to decide the fate of a family, but it was too long a time to stew in their uncertainty.

From then, they had to fiercely agonize over their ideals and reality as if they were being punished.

What kind of situation was it?

Gentle Crow Beggar frowned as he watched Jong Ri-Hyung, who didn’t know what to do.

‘I was wrong.’

Of course, a decision has to be bold. A decision that is postponed and pushed cannot be the right one.

Moreover, from the conversation they had already had, it felt like he could see what decision they were leaning toward.

However, the Gentle Crow Beggar couldn’t criticize them.

It was undeniable that there had been subtle friction between the Nine Great Sects and the Five Great Families. Would they have the loyalty to risk their lives to save the Namgung family?

Not only risking their lives but the fate of the sect too?

This was something Gentle Crow Beggar also wanted to turn down.

He turned his gaze inward, confronting the cynicism and self-loathing that resided within him, and then looked out at Plum Blossom Island.

‘Namgung family lord. How could you do such a foolish thing?’

A deep sigh escaped his lips.

Yet, the Namgung family lord wasn’t entirely to blame. Those present also felt as though their hearts were being carved with a knife. How much more agonizing must it have been for the Namgung family lord to endure that situation on Plum Blossom Island with his family?

It was an extremely harsh price to pay for a single mistake.

Gentle Crow Beggar, who had remained silent until now, spoke with an emotionless expression.

“I think it would be wise for you to remember one thing.”

The abbot and Jong Ri-Hyung turned their attention to him.

“Whatever your choice is, it is up to you two to make it. However...”

Gentle Crow Beggar bit his lip tightly before continuing.

“The world will remember the choice you two make here. And history, beyond this current world, will remember it.”

“....”

“I hope you make the right choice.”

With that, he turned away. It was too difficult to face them any longer.

It wasn’t out of hatred towards them. It was because of the hatred he felt towards himself when he saw them.

‘What about me?’

Wasn’t he also among those who agreed with them? Perhaps he was the most cowardly person there, leaving the decision to them while merely speaking of righteousness.

As Gentle Crow Beggar retreated, a fellow beggar followed.

“Should we report the situation to the main sect?”

“... yes.”

But with only three days left, the main sect wouldn’t be able to make a decision in time. It was indeed a harsh choice for their Beggars Union leader, who was old and had little time left to live.

“Then I will do so.”

“Wait...”

At that moment, Gentle Crow Beggar bit his lip.

He knew it was something he absolutely should not do, but he had no choice. As an elder of the Beggars Union, it was a path he shouldn’t have chosen, but as an individual, he ended up saying it.

“... where did you say Mount Hua was?”

Chapter 953: Do Not Bow Your Head. (3)

“A bit more....”

Namgung Do-Wei bit his lips so hard that they were tearing apart.

“A bit more strength. Just a little... a little longer works....”

He knew. He was pushing his internal qi insanely into the hand that felt like it was falling... but he already understood that no matter how much he tried, he couldn’t save him.

This wasn’t the first time.

It wasn’t the first time he had seen this damned situation.

His hand, stained with dried blood and turned dark red, trembled as it moved to the face.

“Young... Young lord...”

“Yes, Jin. Just endure a bit more. Just hold on a little longer, and the reinforcements will come! Then you can head to Namgung Manor! Until then... keep....”

Namgung Do-Wei’s face contorted horribly.

Even though he knew he shouldn’t do that, he couldn’t stop his face from contorting.

It was an obvious lie. Both the teller and the listener knew it. That was how ridiculous the lie was.

But Namgung Do-Wei desperately told that obvious lie. There was nothing else he could do.

“Young... young... young lord....”

“Namgung Jin...”

“M-make sure... to live...”

“...”

Namgung Do-Wei’s hands were trembling.

He should be used to this by now. He had already sent so many people there. But he couldn’t get used to it at all. No matter how many times he felt the cold, horrid sensation of their body and strength draining from the hand he was holding, it was as painful as the first time.

“I-I want to see... N-Namgung... house... once more....”

Namgung Jin’s eyes, which had been struggling to even get the words out, slowly dilated. At the same time, the strength in Namgung Do-Wei’s hand, which he had been holding onto, slowly vanished.

“Namgung Jin!”

“Namgung Jin! You bastard!”

A fierce scream erupted from those who were guarding him in his last moments. A voice filled with tears and anger, unable to hold back the rising frustration.

However, Namgung Do-Wei simply lowered his head and buried his forehead in Namgung Jin’s chest, where the blood had dried.

‘I shouldn’t cry.’

Blood flowed from his torn lip. Namgung Do-Wei could bleed for Namgung Jin, but he couldn’t allow himself to shed tears.

Because if he shed tears, then everyone would fall.

With bloodshot eyes, Namgung Do-Wei clawed at the ground as if he wanted to tear it apart.

Couldn’t they be saved?

Couldn’t they really have been helped?

If only they had received proper treatment from a skilled physician in time, they might have survived. No, if they had taken the spiritual pills that were so widely available within the family, they might have managed to save a few, if not all.

But there was nothing there.

No one to treat them, no spiritual pills to prolong their lives.

All that remained was a desolate land and an exhausting river that flowed indifferently. And...

Namgung Do-Wei raised his head, his eyes filled with resentment. He glared at the Four Precepts Alliance ships that surrounded the island and the Justice faction sects watching from across the river.

“Uh...”

The anger in his eyes might not have been directed at the Four Precepts Alliance but rather at the Nine Great Sects standing beyond them.

He knew.

All of this was the Namgung Family’s doing. It would be wrong to pass the blame for the consequences onto others. The Namgung family must bear full responsibility for the countless deaths.

But even so...

Clench.

Namgung Do-Wei clenched his teeth.

If they hadn’t shouted and screamed, he wouldn’t have resented them. If they hadn’t brazenly claimed to be protecting the Central Plains and elevating their value, he would have left them alone.

But... where were those people who spoke of righteousness?

What on earth were they doing while those who were injured fighting for the civilians lay dying on the cold, hard ground?

“Uhhh....”

At that moment, a noise pierced the air, gripping the nerves of Namgung Do-Wei. It was the groan of another person struggling with their injury.

He let out a long breath through his chapped lips, then slowly reached out and closed Namgung Jin’s eyes before rising to his feet.

He looked down at Namgung Jin silently and spoke in a dry voice.

“Collect the body.”

“... Young lord.”

“I know how you feel. But there are still those who haven’t recovered. If there is time to mourn their deaths, it should be spent saving the living.”

Who wouldn’t be filled with anger at those words?

Yet none of the red-eyed Namgung family swordsmen objected.

Namgung Do-Wei had been particularly close to Namgung Jin. His slightly trembling shoulders revealed why he was saying such things.

Namgung Do-Wei turned and walked away.

“Young lord....”

The person who had been trying to call him out reflexively trailed off. He knew that Namgung Do-Wei desperately needed time alone.

Namgung Do-Wei walked weakly to the riverside and collapsed there.

The fleet of the Four Precepts Alliance, seemingly indifferent to the situation on Plum Blossom Island, slowly moved while maintaining their position, and the Nine Great Sects stood there.

When he looked back, he saw Namgung Jin’s body being moved and the sight of those treating the injured who hadn’t yet regained consciousness. There was no strength in their hands.

He could understand it.

The suspicion and fear that all of those actions might be meaningless would continue to wear them down.

How many more days were left?

Three days? Two days?

A new, empty laugh escaped through his chapped lips.

If the Ten Thousand People Clan and the pirates were to push into Plum Blossom Island together, then they all would have to die without even a proper fight. In the five days that Jang Ilso had given them, they wouldn’t even be able to build up their strength; all they were was exhausted.

“Kuaak....”

Namgung Do-Wei roughly rubbed his pale face, his hands clutching it as if he would tear it apart, filled with agony.

He knew.

They wouldn’t help the Namgung family. Everyone knew that not just Namgung Do-Wei.

But what really drove Namgung Do-Wei mad was... even though he knew they wouldn’t help him, even though he knew it deep in his bones, he couldn’t give up his last hope.

“Uh...”

A voice escaped from his mouth like a groan. He looked across the river with red eyes.

“... How does it feel?”

His mumbling voice seemed to carry the scent of blood.

“How does it feel to watch this place from there...?”

He wanted to ask.

He wanted to ask them so badly.

Do you know what it feels like to see your family, who you had known until yesterday, die right before your eyes? Do you know what it feels like to see someone whose flesh was rotting away and crying out in pain but not being able to give them even a little help?

Namgung Do-Wei must have felt the same way.

If he hadn’t experienced this, seen it with his own eyes, and groaned in pain, he would have racked his brains on the other side and chosen the plan that would be most beneficial to Namgung.

But now he knew.

‘You cannot consider profit over people’s lives.’

Anyone who has experienced something like this would think the same. Anyone!

“Euh...”

But what can he do?

He realized it too late.

It was at that moment when Namgung Do-Wei’s body, covered in blood and tears, tilted as if it was going to fall.

He suddenly supported himself with his hand on the ground. His piercing eyes, which had no time left, widened significantly.

“... Uh...?”

In the midst of his blurred vision, he could clearly see it.

He roughly rubbed his eyes, then opened them wide again and looked across the river.

Clench!

Namgung Do-Wei bit his lips. His hazy mind suddenly turned clear.

The Nine Great Sects standing across the way probably wouldn’t see it. They wouldn’t even have the time to look to the side or behind them. But Namgung Do-Wei, looking at the riverside, could clearly see it.

The riverside was quite far from where the Nine Great Sects were. Someone wearing green clothes was observing the place.

‘Tang family!’

It was definitely the Sichuan Tang family. Was the Tang family’s warrior, who was already known to have withdrawn from the Yangtze River, checking out this place?

Namgung Do-Wei instantly grabbed his thigh.

‘No, no...’

It was a rushed thought. They might have just sent one person to the Yangtze River to check the condition.

But...

Namgung Do-Wei sat there like a stone statue without moving an inch, looking at the Tang family’s warrior. He carefully observed the movements of the Four Precepts Alliance and the Justice faction, as well as the situation inside Plum Blossom Island, before turning around and walking away.

After a while, Namgung Do-Wei got up as if possessed. He stared into the distance for a long time as if trying to determine whether what he had just seen was reality or an illusion.

‘If the Tang family is here, then...’

After a long time, he slowly turned around.

While walking back the way he came, the entire situation on the island came into view.

“Get up! Get yourself together!”

“Damn it... why are we having to...”

“Kuakk!”

Those who were dying day by day in sorrow. Those who could not hold back their anger while cleaning up the injured. The smell of rotten blood and the stench of corpses. And the stench of despair.

Namgung Do-Wei didn’t stop walking.

‘This is so stupid.’

He knew.

‘It might not yield any results. The situation would only get worse.’

He knew it, too.

‘Then why?’

The reason was simple. He couldn’t just sit there and wait for death to come. He had a responsibility to protect those around him.

He was the young lord of the Namgung family.

His steps quickened with urgency. Finally, he arrived near Namgung Hwang, who appeared to have aged ten years.

Namgung Do-Wei quietly observed Namgung Hwang.

Namgung Hwang drained from attempting to transfer his internal qi to the injured, leaned against a rock shattered by cannon fire, his eyes closed. His face bore the weight of deep regret and a heavy sense of responsibility.

A suffocating burden pressed upon him.

Sensing a presence, Namgung Hwang slowly opened his eyes and looked at Namgung Do-Wei.

“... What is it?”

Namgung Do-Wei stared silently at Namgung Hwang. Father and son locked eyes.

“Didn’t I mention it before?”

“... What?”

“If you can make up your mind, the Lord can escape from the island.”

Namgung Hwang shook his head.

“I will not go.”

A sneering smile crossed his lips.

“Where would I, the Lord of the Namgung family, abandon my family and go? I will share the same fate. And... cough.”

He coughed dryly, his lips twisting.

“I might have considered it before, but now it is impossible.”

“Alone.”

Namgung Hwang slightly raised his head to look at Namgung Do-Wei.

“... What are you trying to say?”

Finally, Namgung Do-Wei, having made up his mind, glared at Namgung Hwang.

“Father, it would be difficult for you to do this alone, but if you join forces with the elders, you could likely get one person off this island.”

“... Do-Wei?”

“Jang Ilso is from the Evil Sect, but he is not one to go back on his word. He has his own principles, so even if we attempt to escape, he will block our escape but not retaliate against the island. In other words...”

Namgung Do-Wei continued his voice firm.

“It would be worth sending one person off.”

“...”

“Please let me go, Lord.”

His eyes burned with determination and anger.

“Please leave me, and let me reach the mainland, even if it means sacrificing my life.”

Namgung Hwang, who had turned pale, looked at his son.

One day, a father will face the moment when his child stands on the same level as him. And right now, Namgung Hwang felt exactly what that moment was like.

That is why he didn’t ask.

What his son was planning to do, or why it had to be Namgung Do-Wei instead of him.

It was all meaningless.

“... and the price?”

He only wondered about that.

“As you said, this is something that all the elders, including me, have to risk their lives for. What is the price we have to pay for giving up our lives when we have to protect everyone’s lives for even a moment longer?”

Namgung Do-Wei’s eyes were filled with determination. It was something that could not be found in Namgung Hwang now.

“Hope.”

“...”

“Wouldn’t that be okay?”

Namgung Hwang, who had been silent, ended up laughing.

“You won’t?”

“....”

Namgung Hwang slowly got up. His eyes began to bloom as well. A firm determination like that of his son who had grown up.

“... that works.”

His hand tightly grasped Namgung Do-Wei’s shoulder.

Chapter 954: Do Not Bow Your Head. (4)

“Umm.”

Jang Ilso snorted and lifted his glass. The bright, full moon illuminated his pale face.

“Late night, on the Yangtze River....”

His lips formed a soft curve.

“Drinking alone with the moon as my companion, how could it not be exciting?”

The moon seemed to quietly descend into his glass.

“... I am right next to you.”

“Tsk.”

Jang Ilso looked back at Ho Ga-Myung, who was sitting across from him, with an annoyed expression.

“You never understand what style is.”

“I don’t know what style is, but I know the rules.”

“Enough.”

Jang Ilso waved his hand and slowly emptied the glass in his hand. After downing every drop, he placed the glass on the table with a slightly languid expression.

“Isn’t this good?”

Looking at Jang Ilso, who seemed to be in a good mood as if he had come out to see the moon, Ho Ga-Myung sighed.

“Of course, the alliance lord is interested, but not me.”

“Why? Are you worried about something?”

“Worried?”

Ho Ga-Myung looked around with a trembling face. The place where they were drinking was none other than the deck of the Black Dragon Ship.

The Black Dragon King was the lord of the ship, and Jang Ilso was the lord of the Alliance... The Black Dragon Ship was a space that symbolized the pirates. Playing around on that deck as if it were his own home was no different from barging into someone else’s living room with his feet on the ground.

‘Even I am the same.’

If the Black Dragon King were to invade the Ten Thousand People Clan and sit on the throne of Jang Ilso, there would be nothing Ho Ga-Myung wouldn’t do to kill the Black Dragon King right away.

‘Can this be called bold?’

That too on this river?

And the Ten Thousand People Clan members they brought were staying on a different ship, not this one. This meant that if the Black Dragon King had any desire, he could be in serious danger.

But Jang Ilso was calmly sipping his drink, which meant that such things didn’t matter to him.

“Kukukuk.”

Jang Ilso laughed softly as he watched Ho Ga-Myung anxiously looking around.

“Ga-Myung, why are you so scared?”

“... If my life were at stake, I wouldn’t be scared. But I am not a great person who would show courage while risking the Alliance Lord’s life.”

“Courage...”

Jang Ilso waved his finger left and right.

“No, no. This isn’t courage; it’s calculation.”

“....”

“The Black Dragon King is smarter than you think. That’s why he absolutely cannot touch me.”

“Why...”

“If I die, the Four Precepts Alliance will lose its leader, and each of them will split up again. Then who will have to bear the anger of those glaring at me with their angry eyes?”

“Ah...”

Ho Ga-Myung nodded slowly.

Certainly, if Jang Ilso backed out, even if the Southern Lands were not involved, the Yangtze River would be scorched. The wrath of Shaolin would be terrifying.

“The Black Dragon King is surely restless, worried that I might die from a stray arrow. So don’t worry about it and just drink. It’s so lonely drinking alone.”

“... You said you drink with the moon as your friend.”

“A good memory. Such a bad habit.”

Jang Ilso snickered and slowly turned his head. He could see the figure of Plum Blossom Island in the darkness at a glance.

“Don’t you feel bad?”

“Are you saying this again?”

Ho Ga-Myung slowly drank the alcohol in front of him.

Tak.

Then he put the glass down on the table and said with an indifferent face.

“Compared to what we have been through, something like this is no crisis.”

“It isn’t something I experienced.”

“....?”

“It is something you and I have experienced.”

At Jang Ilso’s words, a faint smile crossed Ho Ga-Myung’s expressionless face.

People only saw the glamorous side of Jang Ilso.

At a young age, he became the leader of the Ten Thousand People Clan and elevated that clan to become a member of the Shenzhou Five Clans. Now, beyond leading the Ten Thousand People Clan, he had ascended to the position of leader of the Four Precepts Alliance, which oversaw the world’s Evil Sect.

It was an incredibly splendid history.

But who else knew?

What path Jang Ilso and Ho Ga-Myung have walked to achieve such splendid things.

Surviving a near-death experience was not even a drinking snack now. For them, a life-threatening crisis was as natural as the night that came after the sunset.

Ho Ga-Myung picked up the bottle and filled Jang Ilso’s empty glass again.

“And you will experience it in the future.”

“Right. Hahaha.”

Jang Ilso laughed cheerfully and emptied his glass full of alcohol in one gulp. Even so, his eyes were fixed on the Plum Blossom Island in darkness.

“Hmm?”

At that moment, Jang Ilso’s eyes twitched slightly. Ho Ga-Myung hardened his face at that reaction and asked.

“Is there a problem?”

“No.”

But Jang Ilso shook his head.

“It is nothing.”

His lips, red as blood, drew a line.

“And the preparations?”

“... All done.”

Namgung Hwang turned to face Namgung Do-Wei with a stiff face. The son who seemed young forever was now staring with a face of innocence gone.

Namgung Hwang nodded his head sharply.

It didn’t matter if he wasn’t his son. Anyone who could make that kind of expression would be worth risking their life for.

“Elders?”

“Ready, Lord.”

The elders standing behind Namgung Do-Wei were the same.

He looked haggard and thin, and it wouldn’t have been odd if he collapsed, but his two eyes were bright and clear. It was so bright that he worried that he might be discovered by the others.

Namgung Hwang let out a short breath.

“Do-Wei.”

“Yes, Lord.”

“I know you made up your mind, but I request you for the last time.”

“Yes.”

“Do not look back.”

Namgung Do-Wei clenched his fists.

“What is on your shoulders is the life of the Namgung family on this island. Compared to that, our lives are nothing.”

“... I know.”

Namgung Hwang nodded after finishing his words. Then, he quietly looked at Namgung Do-Wei with eyes filled with determination.

“....”

Namgung Hwang, who had engraved the image of his son in his eyes for the last time, turned his body around as if he had no regrets left.

“Namgung Myung.”

“L-Lord.”

“You will handle this place.”

Namgung Myung gritted his teeth. A firm look quickly appeared on his face.

“Yes, leave this to me.”

“Nice.”

Namgung Hwang spoke forcefully without looking back.

“Let’s go.”

“Yes!”

“We will approach without being detected as much as possible.”

There were exactly seven of them: Namgung Hwang, Namgung Do-Wei, and the five elders who were no different from Namgung’s core members.

They began to slip silently into the Yangtze River, which was as black as ink.

They dove under the surface without even making a splashing sound and slowly approached the ship.

Namgung Do-Wei followed right behind Namgung Hwang and carefully swam.

Namgung Do-Wei swam for a long time in the water where he couldn’t even see ahead, and a vain hope suddenly pierced his heart. Maybe he could pass under the ship without being detected and reach the land.

But the wind was just wind, and hopes were usually shattered.

Before they were even halfway to the ship, something rose from the bottom.

“Kuak!”

Pure white bubbles poured out of Namgung Hwang’s mouth.

Those insane pirates were watching the riverbed to see if anyone was moving in and out of there.

If they were out of the water, there was no way they could detect Namgung Hwang’s presence. But this was underwater. In other words, there was not a single place to hide.

Grruk!

A loud noise rang out from underwater, and dozens of spears flew toward them.

Namgung Hwang gritted his teeth and drew his sword.

Kwaaang!

A massive splash of water erupted above the calm surface of the Yangtze River.

“What is that?”

“The enemy! The rats are in the water!”

“Arrows!”

The once tranquil Yangtze River was suddenly filled with shouts and curses.

Kwang!

The Black Dragon King, who had been inside the cabin, jumped out swiftly.

“What is happening?”

“It looks like they are trying to escape!”

“What!?”

The Black Dragon King quickly turned his head and looked toward Plum Blossom Island. As he widened his eyes, he could clearly see people groaning in the center of the island.

‘What is this?’

Most of the people were still on the island.

Then?

“Namgung Hwang!”

The Black Dragon King roared and ran across the deck.

Swish!

Soon, the form of the Emperor Sword, Namgung Hwang, rising from the water, came into view.

“Euhahahahahah! This little rat! Does he want to live?”

The Black Dragon King burst into laughter and waved his hand in the air as if he had no time to waste.

“Shoot! Turn that coward into a hedgehog now!”

The pirates swarmed to one side of the deck, causing the ship to tilt. They were packed so tightly that their shoulders touched, all raising their bows toward Namgung Hwang.

“Fireee!”

Swish!

Arrows loaded with qi were released all at once. Not only did the Black Dragon King fire, but arrows from dozens of ships also covered Namgung Hwang’s body.

If it had been daytime instead of night, the arrows would have cast a huge shadow over the Yangtze River.

“Black Dragon KINGGGG!”

But at that moment...

Kwaaaang!

The enormous sword qi Namgung Hwang released shone like the sun rising through the river, illuminating the surrounding area. The rain of arrows collided with the sky-high sword qi and bounced off in all directions.

The sword qi was so impressive that it gave the onlookers goosebumps.

“Hahahahah!”

The Black Dragon King burst into laughter at the sight.

“Tear it apart! Let him know exactly what the price is for daring to dip his body into the Yangtze River!”

The pirates dropped their bows as if abandoning them and jumped into the water. There were so many of them that the foam rising as they jumped made the river look white for a moment.

Chak!

A multitude of people rushed through the water at incredible speed, cutting through the current and heading toward Namgung Hwang and the seven others.

Not only outside the water but inside it as well.

It was like a school of sharks rushing toward a tiger that had fallen into the water.

“Lord!”

“I know!”

Namgung Hwang shouted loudly.

“Elders, stop those approaching from inside the water! I will take charge of the surface!”

He gripped the handle of his sword so tightly that his knuckles turned white.

‘I will make sure that Do-Wei lives!’

A strong will never seen before surged in his eyes.

“Come! Dogs of pirates! I will show you how the lord of the Namgung family dies!”

Namgung Hwang’s sword roared like a wounded tiger and struck the water’s surface with tremendous force.

Kwaaak!

Huge ripples began to form on the surface of the Yangtze River. The rising waves shook the ships floating on the water.

And at that moment.

Paat!

The mercenaries, floating above the water like dolphins, hurled spears at Namgung Hwang and Namgung Do-Wei.

“Do-Wei!”

“Yes!”

Tat!

Namgung Do-Wei stepped on Namgung Hwang’s shoulder and jumped out of the water like a flying bird. His sword drew a pure white line in the air.

Dark sky.

Pitch-black river.

The white line in the middle looked pitiful.

Chapter 955: Do Not Bow Your Head. (5)

Paaat!

Namgung Do-Wei’s sword mercilessly cut through the bodies of the pirates rushing toward him. There was not even a shred of hesitation. The venom at the tip of the sword spoke of Namgung Do-Wei’s determination.

And at that moment, Namgung Hwang burst out from beneath him.

“Haaah!”

Kwaaak!

The dazzling white sword qi that rose up swept away the pirates rushing in from the front.

As expected.

No, this word is not enough to describe the force.

“Come! You dirty pirates!”

A storm-like roar erupted. Namgung Hwang’s eyes were the only ones clear on the dark river as if they were burning.

Swish!

The spear flew fiercely.

Kakang!

Namgung Hwang cut the flying harpoon in half, but he didn’t have the time to cut down the pirate who threw it. Before he could even swing the sword again, another harpoon flew in, and before that could reach Namgung Hwang, another followed.

In the first place, a harpoon was a weapon for hunting, not for war.

The harpoons were much thinner than spears and wouldn’t get caught. The hook on the end could not be pulled out without tearing the flesh once it pierced through.

When warriors faced off against each other, it was a weapon that was disadvantageous, but when hunting a small number of people like this, the harpoon was more powerful than any other weapon.

The madness in the eyes of the pirates sparkled even in the darkness. More than a dozen harpoons flew toward Namgung Hwang and Namgung Do-Wei with all their might.

“Don’t underestimate them!”

Namgung Do-Wei kicked the water surface and jumped back up, spraying out sword qi at the pirates rushing with harpoons.

Iron Sword Twelve Forms!

Unlike the Emperor Sword form, which requires great strength, this is the basic sword form of the Namgung family. The simple sword style was especially effective in situations like this.

It was quick because it was simple and powerful because it was quick. The white sword qi cut through and shattered the flying harpoons, embedding itself into the pirates’ bodies.

“Ackkkkk!”

“Kuak!”

The pirates screamed and fell. The blood they spilled spread across the Yangtze River, but the darkness that had settled only indifferently consumed their blood and corpses.

Paaat!

However, even though their comrades were cut down and died, the flying harpoons did not cease.

“Ohhhhh!”

This time, Namgung Hwang shouted and swung his sword. The sword qi that surged up exploded, creating huge waves in all directions. The pirates who had risen above the water were swept away by the terrifying waves and pushed back.

But...

“Kill them!!”

“They are fewer in number! Kill them all!”

“I will cut off the Emperor Sword’s head!”

Even if they killed and killed again, more would rush in, and even if they pushed them away repeatedly, more would chase after them.

No, it wasn’t just that. Even though their comrades were dying right in front of them, the pirates continued to fight like hungry sharks smelling blood.

“Shooot!”

Paaat!

At that moment, hundreds of arrows shot out from several ships slowly approaching them. The rain of arrows, which seemed as if it would block out the sky, changed direction all at once and began to pour down on Namgung Hwang and Namgung Do-Wei’s heads.

“These insane people...!”

A roar burst from Namgung Hwang’s mouth.

Kwkwang! Kakang!

The arrows carrying the qi pierced at an incredible speed, but Namgung Hwang was not one to be hit by such crude arrows. The ones who were hurt by those arrows were none other than the pirates rushing at the two.

“Cough!”

“Ack!”

The arrows that pierced the back of the pirate rose up in front of his face. Those who rushed ahead without thought were unable to dodge the arrows and, in an instant, turned into hedgehogs.

“This...!”

Namgung Hwang’s eyes burned with intensity.

They shot arrows even though he was aware his comrades were present. They must have known that no matter how many arrows they launched, they would only provoke Namgung Hwang to slash back!

Were they trying to suggest that their lives were less valuable than Namgung Hwang’s?

“Black Dragon KIINNNGGGGG!”

“Shoot!”

Namgung Hwang let out a roar of anger, but the Black Dragon King remained composed, calmly issuing instructions.

“It is fine if you don’t inflict wounds. Just drain their qi; that will suffice.”

Essentially, all sword techniques originated from the lower body. In a place where there was no ground to step on, it was challenging for any sword to exert its full force. This meant that to achieve its normal strength, it had to expend twice the normal amount of internal qi.

Especially for Namgung Hwang, who channeled a significant amount of internal qi into each sword form and move, he would soon tire. Particularly since he had been exerting himself on Plum Blossom Island for some time and was unable to maintain his form.

Once Namgung Hwang’s qi was sufficiently drained, the Black Dragon King would advance and sever his head.

“Moron.”

A bitter smile formed on the lips of the Black Dragon King.

He didn’t know what Namgung Hwang was thinking, but from the moment he plunged into the river of his own accord, Namgung Hwang’s fate was sealed.

Bang! Bang! Bang!

The arrows, charged with intense qi, pierced the surface and dug deep.

Those arrows didn’t cause any real harm to Namgung Hwang. But that was limited to Namgung Hwang.

Those who were fighting the arrows on the water could block them with ease, but the elders battling the pirates underwater were in an entirely different situation.

As they looked down at the water’s surface to deal with the pirates below, arrows suddenly struck them. The arrows, loaded with qi, did not lose their power even after hitting the water, and their fate was sealed as they faced dozens of such arrows coming at them.

Crack!

The Namgung family’s name, and being an elder there were not in vain; they instantly swatted off the arrows, but it was impossible to block them all. The sharp tips dug into the back and sides of one.

Crack!

“Third Elder!”

A scream-like shout burst out from Namgung Hwang.

The Third Elder, flinching at the arrows piercing his body, instead dove deeper into the water.

Namgung Hwang clenched his teeth.

There was no time to take care of himself. The moment they left the battle line, Namgung Hwang would have to deal with the harpoons flying from the water as well as below.

Knowing that the Third Elder chose to fight the pirates without caring for his own life.

Namgung Hwang looked around.

It was endless.

It was hard to tell whether he was looking at the river or the pirates in the river.

Namgung Hwang clenched his teeth.

“Do-Wei!”

“Yes!”

“Behind my back!”

Namgung Do-Wei looked at Namgung Hwang for a moment. Namgung Do-Wei opened his mouth as if he was going to say something but soon bit his lips until they bled and gulped down his words.

The moment he kicked the water and moved behind Namgung Hwang, a heavy breath escaped Namgung Hwang’s mouth.

“Do-Wei!”

“Yes, lord!”

In the midst of the confusion, Namgung Hwang glanced at Do-Wei. Namgung Hwang’s mouth opened as he looked at him with eyes that were hard to understand.

“Do not fall... behind your father.”

“....”

That was all he said. Namgung Hwang grabbed his sword once again.

If they were caught in this place, the result was obvious. However, what they had to do was not endure it but move ahead.

At any cost!

“Elders! Protect Do-Wei!”

His voice might have pierced through the water and reached down below. However, they would have understood Namgung Hwang’s intentions without him having to say anything.

Kwaaang!

A brilliant light.

Namgung Hwang’s sword qi swept ahead with this brilliant shine, which seemed to instantly turn the dark sky into a blue sky.

The powerful sword qi literally split the Yangtze River in an instant. The waters that filled the path of the sword qi were crushed without even being able to scream.

“Let’s go!”

The river shook like a stormy sea as the water, which had been pushed back, surged forward. Namgung Hwang kicked the water and advanced in a straight line.

“Die!”

“You monster!”

The pirates rolled their heads and rushed at Namgung Hwang.

Another sword strike!

Kwaak!

The sword qi Namgung Hwang released slammed into the pirates along with the river water.

Swish! Trash!

It felt like someone was stabbing his dantian with a large needle. He wasn’t even in good shape to start with, and he had jumped into the river. In that situation, it was only natural that his stamina would run out with him constantly using sword qi.

But Namgung Hwang didn’t think about what would happen next.

There was no turning back. Only forward.

“OHHHH!”

Namgung Hwang’s sword moved greatly once again. The white sword qi that flashed and bloomed brilliantly seemed to draw the path they would take on the river.

“Come!”

Kwaaang!

The floor of the river, struck by the sword qi, burst apart as if a giant hand had torn it apart.

“Come now!”

Namgung Hwang stretched his neck and roared.

His body had no more strength left in it. Now, his dantian was as painful as if it had been slashed with a sword.

Nevertheless, his mind was calmer than before he jumped into the water.

The sword qi was released again.

‘Now... I understand.’

He sometimes pondered about the Emperor Sword technique, which was half-baked.

The extreme attack-oriented technique was a peculiar sword style that used an unbelievable amount of internal qi to produce immense power. However, it left the user extremely vulnerable from behind.

This was why the Namgung family always produced some of the best swordsmen in the world, yet they never overcame the final hurdle—the title of the best swordsman in the world was never theirs.

Why did his ancestors choose this sword technique to represent Namgung?

But...

‘I understand now.’

There was no need to protect his back.

Because his son was now behind him.

The Emperor’s Sword. The Sword of the Emperor.

The sword wasn’t for show. It wasn’t to defeat the pirates.

It was to protect those who followed while watching his back.

That was the image of a monarch that Namgung desired.

‘No... I suppose it is the way of the family’s lord.’

His sword spoke.

It told him to give everything he had left to protect his son.

The corners of Namgung Hwang’s mouth lifted, revealing his white teeth. The surging waves continued to hit his face, and the bitter river water rushed into his throat, yet his eyes were clearer than ever.

“Do-Wei!”

“Yes!”

“Watch me closely!”

This is the path he was taking. And the path his son would take one day.

Kwaaak!

The terrifying sword qi he released swept away and struck down the pirates charging at him relentlessly.

Crack!

But then, a harpoon flew through the imperfect sword qi and pierced his side.

Namgung Hwang didn’t even glance at it.

Paat!

He immediately decapitated the pirate who threw it, then successively cut down the throats of the surrounding, frightened pirates.

Crack! Crack!

Another harpoon from the water pierced his leg, and a shower of arrows struck his shoulder.

Nevertheless, Namgung Hwang stared ahead.

‘Open up.’

Even if it meant burning everything down.

The path that Namgung would walk on.

The path that his son would have to take.

Chapter 956: Do Not Bow Your Head. (6)

Kwaang!

The light kept flashing. The calm Yangtze River was now filled with loud noises and screams.

Even the ships were moving rapidly, so it was impossible not to know what was happening there.

Kuak.

The Shaolin disciples standing on the riverbank clenched their fists. Their bodies, leaning forward, seemed as if they would kick the ground and run ahead at any moment.

But that was impossible.

The back of the abbot at the front was tightly holding them down.

Euk.

The Shaolin monks, gritting their teeth, watched the situation with bloodshot eyes. No, it would be more accurate to say they were glaring.

In the middle of the river, where they couldn’t set foot, a few people by the name of Namgung were struggling like sheep being torn apart by a pack of wolves.

“Abbot!”

Someone shouted. But the abbot didn’t even look back and stared ahead with cold eyes.

“Abbot, shouldn’t we help them?”

“Wait.”

“Abbot!”

“Didn’t I tell you to wait?”

The abbot answered with a shout.

“Even Namgung hasn’t shown his full potential! So why should we shed blood in Namgung’s place?”

“...”

“Can you see that Ten Thousand People aren’t moving? If Jang Ilso and the Black Dragon King stepped in, this would have been handled already! Why do you think they are just watching?”

“Ugh....”

The abbot bit his lip.

“If we are lured in, it is all over. Not just them, but also those who remain on Plum Blossom Island and even us may suffer.”

The sound of someone gritting their teeth echoed.

They could go. If they wanted, they could move to help them whenever they wished.

That was why it was more painful. They had to just watch and ignore even though the others were within arm’s reach.

Someone lowered their head as if they couldn’t bear to see the sight ahead.

“Do not lower your head!”

At that moment, a harsh shout rang out from Hae Bang’s mouth.

“S-sasuk....”

“Look carefully, look with your own eyes! You don’t even dare to look now! How cowardly can you be!”

“Hae Bang!”

The abbot’s words of anger burst out, but Hae Bang didn’t move an inch. He just stared at the river, his eyes wide as if he wanted to engrave the scene in his memory.

Beop Gye closed his eyes.

‘How did things come to this?’

They had clearly been filled with the will to save the Namgung family until they stepped here, but now he could feel a crack within them. An unbridgeable gap had formed between those who commanded and those who obeyed.

The gaze of Beop Gye, which opened with difficulty, turned to the river. The white qi in the dark, which Namgung Hwang was pouring out, felt so cold.

Chaak!

The tip of the sword turned dull. To be exact, the arm swinging the sword turned dull. Namgung Hwang’s sword was no longer sharp or powerful.

But...

Slash!

“Gruk....”

It was alright. His sword existed when it should, where it should.

The blood spilled by the pirates turned even the dark river red. Even the occasional splash of foam was not white but red.

Namgung Hwang continued to advance in the red-stained river.

Paat!

A dozen or so harpoons flew at him.

Blocking them? Impossible.

Namgung Hwang swung the sword without hesitation. The first thing his sword struck was not the harpoon aimed at the most dangerous vital point but the harpoon that could pass over him and reach Do-Wei.

Kakakakng!

The harpoon bounced off Namgung Hwang’s sword, but the two that he couldn’t hit dug into his body without fault.

The harpoons pierced the skin, tore the muscles, and embedded themselves there.

“Lord!”

As Namgung Do-Wei shouted, Namgung Hwang’s sword swung and cut off the necks of the pirates who were stabbing him with the harpoons.

“I can block that much! I....”

“Do-Wei.”

“...”

Namgung Do-Wei went silent.

There was no strength in his voice. It wasn’t the voice that was always loud and naturally made people listen to him.

But... the tenacity in his voice was incomparable to the past.

“... see it through till the end.”

That was all. Namgung Hwang, who had spoken with his gaze fixed ahead, began to move forward.

The blood flowing from his head seeped into his eyes, and soon the waves that had surged washed it away.

He couldn’t even tell how much time had passed.

“Woahhhhh!”

“DIE! You monster!”

He kept stabbing and cutting endlessly. He felt something stuck in his thigh, but it was alright now. He had long since lost all feeling below his waist.

‘Elders?’

They were there.

If it wasn’t for them, he wouldn’t have even been alive until now. They were desperately trying to endure as much as they could.

Burning their lives.

The blood that colored this river must also be tainted with their blood. Namgung Hwang burst into laughter as he looked at the red foam in the water.

‘For what is all this?’

For what were they risking their lives?

To atone for bringing Namgung into this crisis?

Or because they wanted to give a ray of hope to the remaining Namgung members on the island?

Well, it could not be known. And none of it mattered now.

Puak! Puak!

The pirates, sensing that Namgung Hwang was losing it, rushed in even more fiercely. The sight of them baring their teeth and rushing in, mixed with the red waves, was like the devils of hell.

At this moment, there was just one thing he had to do.

‘Do-Wei!’

-Wouldn’t that be enough?

How could it not be enough?

A pure white smile formed on Namgung Hwang’s mouth.

WOOONG!

He pulled out all the qi from his trembling lower stomach as if it would tear off, and his internal qi gushed out. He used his dantian, which had been drained, and somehow the internal qi that had flowed down his arm was pushed into the sword.

At that moment, Namgung Hwang felt Namgung Do-Wei’s hand gripping his shoulder. That small hand had grown so much before he realized it.

‘I will open it!’

There was only one thing he had to do.

Get Namgung Do-Wei out of there alive. There was no need to think of anything else.

“Woaahh!”

Namgung Hwang, who had unleashed his lion’s roar with all his might, saw dozens of pirates rushing toward him.

Grabbing his sword, he released his taut muscles and qi in an instant.

KWAAAAAAA!

The white sword qi surged like a giant dragon rising up the Yangtze River.

It was a sword slash that could only be described as terrifying. The pirates who charged at Namgung Hwang were crushed in an instant and scattered across the Yangtze River without even a scream.

But of course, Namgung Hwang wasn’t unharmed either.

Clench.

He broke the harpoon embedded in his chest. There was no way to pull it out without tearing through the flesh.

Crack! Crack! Crack.

He broke the harpoons lodged in his body, chest, stomach, and shoulder, one after another, and looked ahead.

His vision was blurred. His consciousness was gradually fading.

Crack!

What brought him back to his senses was the intense pain he felt in his waist. He swung his sword and cut down the pirate who attacked him from within the water. Then he submerged his head for a moment.

“...”

He could vaguely see the elders sinking deep into the water. He couldn’t discern their expressions.

Just...

Namgung Hwang bit his lip and glared ahead.

“Lord!”

“Go... Do-Wei.”

He didn’t have time to feel sad. He would end up in the same situation as them.

He kicked the water and moved forward.

Cutting off the heads of the rushing pirates and grabbing their harpoons that pierced him.

Cut, cut, cut, and cut.

His hair, disheveled and soaked in water, stuck together badly.

Crack! Crack!

Even though harpoons were lodged into his side and thigh, Namgung Hwang, as if feeling no pain, cut through the pirates and moved ahead.

So much blood poured out that it seemed as if another river had formed atop the existing one.

Namgung Hwang rampaged like an asura from hell. Even the pirates, seasoned by countless battles, were weary of this.

“Uhh...”

“Cough. Cough!”

Namgung Hwang coughed in pain, revealing his blood-soaked teeth. His rough breathing escaped from both his mouth and nose.

It was at the last moment.

Paaat!

A powerful piercing sound, as if tearing through Namgung Hwang’s ears, came. Before his mind could decide, his sword moved instinctively.

Kwaaang!

He collided with a strong attack that flew from above the ship, which immediately threw him back, causing him to spit out blood.

“You damned fools!”

The Black Dragon King roared like a tiger as he stood on the bow of the Black Dragon Ship.

“What are you so afraid of when you have someone dying right in front of you! Charge at them with all your might and make a mess of them!”

“Yes!”

The pirates, crushed by the Black Dragon King’s force, clenched their teeth and moved toward Namgung Hwang again.

“Lord! Father! Damn it! Father!”

Namgung Do-Wei kicked the water and blocked Namgung Hwang, who was floating on it.

His red eyes were filled with determination to never hand Namgung Hwang over to them.

But.

Tak.

Namgung Hwang’s trembling hand rested on Namgung Do-Wei’s shoulder.

“F-father....”

“... Go... away...”

“Father!”

“... hurry.”

His eyes were swollen and nearly closed.

But the desperate light in them was something Namgung Do-Wei couldn’t defy.

“Back... my back...”

Bloody tears flowed from Namgung Do-Wei’s eyes.

He knew. Right.

It was Namgung Do-Wei who had asked them to send him off the island at the cost of their lives. So he could not be mistaken about what was happening.

But... but!

“You... Namgung’s next... Lord.”

“....”

“With me.”

Namgung Do-Wei lowered his head. Then, silently, he parted the water and went behind Namgung Hwang, grabbing his back. Like a child being carried on his father’s back.

“Uh...”

A faint smile formed on Namgung Hwang’s contorted face.

Puak!

He felt something stab his leg. His lower stomach was being pierced. He couldn’t block any more attacks from underwater.

But it was fine.

As long as he carried Namgung Do-Wei, the attacks wouldn’t touch his son.

“Do-Wei.”

Suddenly, a clear voice came.

Namgung Do-Wei grabbed his shoulder tightly.

“F-Father...”

That was the moment.

Kwaang!

Namgung Hwang’s body rose up by kicking the water. He pulled out his innate qi and regained his strength in an instant.

“BLOCK!”

“EHHHH!”

The pirates rose from the water, shouting as if they wanted to tear him apart, and blocked him.

Kwaaang!

Namgung Hwang swung his sword in one swipe, shattering the pirates into pieces and moving ahead, kicking the water.

“OHHH!”

White sword qi shot up all around, spreading like fireworks. The pirates were terrified and dove into the water. It was a ridiculous fight to have against sword qi.

Kwaang!

Namgung Hwang kicked the water, advancing through the ships surrounding him.

One step! Just one more...

It was then.

Kaaak!

A dark-colored blade qi from the Black Dragon Ship rushed straight at Namgung Hwang. The enhanced blade, which had pulled off the white sword qi with all its might, cut through easily and pierced Namgung Hwang’s body.

Crack!

“This...”

The Black Dragon King was about to say something as he watched Namgung Hwang stagger.

Clench!

Namgung Hwang didn’t fall but kicked the water again.

His eyes shone menacingly through the hair clinging to his blood-soaked face.

Drops of blood spattered from his body and scattered in all directions. At that moment, Namgung Hwang grabbed Do-Wei’s arms.

“OHHHHHH!”

Then, with the last of his qi, he threw Namgung Hwang to the riverside.

“Fatherrrrr!”

A desperate scream poured out from Namgung Do-Wei’s mouth.

In his frantic gaze, he saw Namgung Hwang fluttering as if he had lost all his strength and fallen into the river.

In the upside-down world, Namgung Hwang’s face was briefly visible.

He was... surely smiling.

Splash.

Namgung Hwang, thrown into the river, sank down.

As he descended into the dark world, he thought.

‘Is this enough...’

He didn’t know.

But he did his best.

It wasn’t like everything he did was right, but at least he did his best until the very end.

Now, all he had to do was trust and entrust his son, who would carry on the Namgung name.

‘...Do-Wei...’

A smile formed on his lips as he sank into the pitch-black river.

‘... be a good father...’

With that faint smile, Namgung Hwang’s eyes finally closed.

Chapter 957: Where Is Mount Hua? (1)

Splash!

Namgung Do-Wei, who had fallen into the water, clenched his teeth.

‘Father!’

He knew he shouldn’t be sad. There was no time for sadness.

He flailed his arms and legs, focusing only on the riverside, which wasn’t too far away.

“EUHHHH!”

A desperate scream burst from his mouth.

Resentment, anger, sadness, and pain.

Countless emotions mixed together and surged through him like a howl.

He couldn’t tell what was flowing down his face—blood, tears, or river water. He just kept moving his limbs, pushing forward.

If he died there, those who had already perished would have died in vain.

So he had to move!

“Euhhh!”

Namgung Do-Wei, advancing with a howl, had to stop before he could take a couple more steps. The pirates stationed in front surrounded him.

Namgung Do-Wei glared at them with red eyes and drew his sword.

‘Do not get anxious.’

If only he could vent his anger here. He felt like letting go of his reason and swinging his arms until he collapsed from exhaustion.

But that couldn’t happen.

Right now, this wasn’t just his life.

‘I must survive.’

At any cost! At all costs!

Paat!

His sword instantly sliced through the waists of the pirate troops rushing towards him.

Hot blood gushed at him from the torn bodies, covering his face. The cold river and hot blood mingled.

Crack!

A flying harpoon grazed his arm, gouging out the flesh. However, he didn’t have time to feel pain. Namgung Do-Wei swung his sword, stabbing the enemies, striking the surface of the water, and moving ahead.

‘I need to live!’

Crack!

Another harpoon, flying from the water, dug into his stomach. Namgung Do-Wei grabbed and broke the shaft, then swung his sword again.

‘For sure!’

The riverbank wasn’t far now.

Just a little... a little more!

“EUKKKK!”

Every time he swung his sword as if in a frenzy, the white sword qi split the bodies of the pirates. Even as hot blood covered his face, his arms and legs didn’t stop.

‘I need to...’

A bit more...

Kwaang!

At that moment, Namgung Do-Wei turned at the ominous sound from behind.

A silent, enhanced blade qi.

The one that took down Namgung Hwang was coming for him. A powerful blow that his exhausted body could not withstand.

Namgung Do-Wei’s eyes trembled and widened.

‘No...’

Kwaaaaak!

Just then, the enhanced blade qi coming for him exploded in the air. The pirates chasing Namgung Do-Wei screamed as they were swept up by the explosion.

“EUKKKKK!”

“ACKKKKKKKK!”

Namgung Do-Wei was momentarily flustered.

‘What is that?’

What just happened?

But instead of pondering further, he immediately turned around. The important thing now was not ‘how.’ What mattered was survival.

“Get out of my way, you bastards!”

With a desperate scream, Namgung Do-Wei headed for the land.

“This...”

The Black Dragon King was enraged.

“What were you doing!”

His face was filled with rage.

The Black Dragon King had never had to suppress his anger like this before. Normally, if he was this angry, he would rush out and kill them. That was his nature.

But even though he was enraged now, he couldn’t bring himself to rush at the opponent.

The reason was simple.

The one who made him angry right now was none other than the Hegemony Lord, Jang Ilso.

“Why didn’t you let me attack and block it!”

He had clearly aimed to kill the boy. If there had been no interference, Namgung Do-Wei would be dead. But his attack exploded before it could touch Namgung Do-Wei, colliding with the qi of Jang Ilso.

In other words, Jang Ilso saved Namgung Do-Wei.

“Hmm.”

Jang Ilso just snorted and looked at the Black Dragon King with indifference.

“Alliance Lord!”

“Tsk tsk.”

When the Black Dragon King couldn’t hold back and shouted, Jang Ilso just clicked his tongue and glared at him in a mocking manner.

“How can a person be so cruel...”

“... what now?”

Jang Ilso smiled and curled his red lips.

“Isn’t it so sad?”

“...”

“He worked so hard to save his child, and you are killing the father and then going after the child too. That is too heartbreaking.”

The Black Dragon King gritted his teeth.

What nonsense was this?

“Hahahahaha.”

Jang Ilso laughed loudly and glanced at Namgung Do-Wei. He was desperately fighting off the pirates. Jang Ilso’s eyes turned dark.

“Do you not want to see?”

“... see what?”

“The moment he arrives at the riverside and faces Shaolin.”

The Black Dragon King, who was about to say something, went silent. Jang Ilso slowly continued.

“Isn’t that how people are? They tend to hide, cover-up, and ignore what they consider to be their own lacking aspects. But... there will inevitably come a time in life when you have to face your own lacking things.”

“...”

“At that time...”

Jang Ilso licked his lips and looked at the Black Dragon King.

“Do you know what expression a person makes?”

The Black Dragon King trembled slightly at that.

He couldn’t understand what this man’s thoughts were, but he didn’t need to. Jang Ilso’s malice was vividly conveyed in his eyes.

“You will see it now.”

Hearing Jang Ilso’s low voice, the Black Dragon King glared at him. However, as time passed, it was the Black Dragon King who avoided the gaze first.

“Collect the bodies!”

The Black Dragon King, who turned around, gave rough orders.

Ho Ga-Myung, who was listening to the entire talk, shook his head.

He understood how he felt, but it was because he didn’t like that attitude.

‘It will not be long.’

Ho Ga-Myung, who glared at the Black Dragon King, straightened his face and spoke to Jang Ilso.

“Alliance Lord, now...”

“Shh.”

At that moment, Jang Ilso stopped Ho Ga-Myung as if telling him to be quiet and slowly reached out his hand. Then he grabbed the alcohol bottle.

He quietly looked down at the bottle, then extended his hand over the railing and tilted it.

Drizzle.

The flowing alcohol went into the Yangtze River.

Ho Ga-Myung, who understood the meaning of that, looked at Jang Ilso.

“Doesn’t the Lord dislike idiotic people?”

“I do.”

Jang Ilso nodded.

“But I don’t hate them.”

“...”

Before he knew it, calmness fell onto the Yangtze River. Jang Ilso’s slightly sunken eyes looked at the flowing river.

“I achieved what I wanted to, so I have no regrets.”

The alcohol spilled had fallen like rain on the Yangtze River.

He felt the ground under his toes.

Splash.

Namgung Do-Wei stepped ahead with a foot that felt like it carried a thousand pounds.

At this point, the pursuit stopped.

A bloody path appeared in the black river he passed through. At the end of it, Namgung Do-Wei continued to walk, staggering.

The water, which was up to his neck, reached his chest, and soon his upper body was out of the water.

Splash. Splash.

“I am alive...”

He kept mumbling it like he was possessed.

His eyes were half-closed and dark, but Namgung Do-Wei kept moving ahead.

“I need to be alive...”

Splash. Splash.

And soon, his feet emerged from the water.

Tak.

He reached the land and walked slowly. The water from the river poured down from the sword, which came out of the water.

And...

Those standing on the land watched without making a sound. They didn’t dare do anything as they watched him stagger toward them.

Because they felt bad. Because they felt shame.

They didn’t even dare to support him as he staggered.

Thud. Thud.

Finally, Namgung Do-Wei reached them, slammed the ground, and leaned against the sword.

It was a miserable sight.

There was no place on his body that wasn’t wounded, and the water dripping down his body was red, mixed with his blood. And even now, blood was dripping down. As the hem of his clothes turned red, those watching the scene ached.

“Cough! Cough!”

Namgung Do-Wei’s body shook as he coughed, then struggled to regain his place.

Soon, very slowly, so slowly that it felt like an eternity, Namgung Do-Wei raised his head.

Who would dare look him in the eye?

Even though Namgung Do-Wei looked up, no one could meet his eyes. Everyone lowered their gazes as if they had collectively decided to do so.

Even the Shaolin monks stood like statues, observing the entire situation.

The disciples of the Kongtong, who arrived late, couldn’t do anything.

The beggars of the Beggars Union, who prided themselves on living in the lowest positions and moved by emotions, didn’t move.

No one could look Namgung Do-Wei in the eye.

Even the head of Shaolin, the abbot, turned his gaze to the distant sky.

That was the way it would be for a person. Anyone with even a shred of conscience wouldn’t be able to look into those eyes.

“Uhuh...”

A groan escaped from Namgung Do-Wei.

“Hehe....”

And after that came a soft, distorted laugh.

No one knew the meaning of the laugh. They could only guess that it contained self-mockery and sadness.

“Cough! Cough!”

Namgung Do-Wei coughed loudly again and staggered as if he would collapse, but he held the sword tightly and stood tall. Then he took a deep breath.

At that moment, everyone felt terrified.

Whether the words coming out of his mouth were criticism, harsh shouts, or even sarcasm... they couldn’t speak back. They had no choice but to close their eyes and listen.

What would they say if they were Namgung Do-Wei?

The expected words were too painful and too many. They were all painful.

And after that long and short period of suffering... Namgung Do-Wei finally spoke.

They barely resisted the urge to cover their ears at Namgung Do-Wei’s voice.
